\maketitle
\noindent This document contains all 70 questions on \emph{differentiating} $y=f(x)$, each with a fully
worked solution and a TikZ diagram: the curve in blue with its \textbf{derivative} $f'(x)$ in teal for the
pure differentiation questions; the curve with the \textbf{tangent} (dashed orange) at the relevant point
for gradient, tangent, and stationary-point questions; and additionally the \textbf{normal} (dash-dotted
purple) for the normal questions.

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 01\quad\small\texttt{(d2-001)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 01 (d2-001)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 1}

\question{Differentiate \( y = x^5 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Apply the power rule: multiply by the index, then reduce the index by 1.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 5x^{5-1} = 5x^4
\end{gathered}\]
The rule is: if \( y = x^n \), then \( \frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = nx^{n-1} \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 5x^4 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 02\quad\small\texttt{(d2-002)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 02 (d2-002)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 1}

\question{Differentiate \( y = x^7 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Apply the power rule.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 7x^6
\end{gathered}\]
Multiply the coefficient by the power (here coefficient is 1), then reduce the power by 1.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 7x^6 \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 03\quad\small\texttt{(d2-003)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 03 (d2-003)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 1}

\question{Differentiate \( y = 6x^4 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Multiply the coefficient by the power, then reduce the power by 1.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 6 \times 4x^3 = 24x^3
\end{gathered}\]
When there is a number in front, multiply it by the index.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 24x^3 \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 04\quad\small\texttt{(d2-004)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 04 (d2-004)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 2}

\question{Differentiate \( y = \sqrt{x} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Rewrite the square root as a fractional power.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = x^{1/2}
\end{gathered}\]
\( \sqrt{x} = x^{1/2} \). Always convert roots to fractional powers before differentiating.

\textit{Step 2: Apply the power rule.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \tfrac{1}{2}x^{-1/2}
\end{gathered}\]
Multiply by \( \tfrac{1}{2} \) and reduce the index to \( \tfrac{1}{2} - 1 = -\tfrac{1}{2} \).

\textit{Step 3: Rewrite using a surd if required.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \frac{1}{2\sqrt{x}}
\end{gathered}\]
\( x^{-1/2} = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{x}} \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \dfrac{1}{2\sqrt{x}} \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 05\quad\small\texttt{(d2-005)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 05 (d2-005)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 2}

\question{Differentiate \( y = \dfrac{1}{x^3} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Rewrite as a negative power of \( x \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = x^{-3}
\end{gathered}\]
\( \dfrac{1}{x^3} = x^{-3} \). Convert before differentiating.

\textit{Step 2: Apply the power rule.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = -3x^{-4}
\end{gathered}\]
Multiply by \(-3\) and reduce the index: \(-3 - 1 = -4\).

\textit{Step 3: Write in fraction form.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = -\frac{3}{x^4}
\end{gathered}\]
\( x^{-4} = \dfrac{1}{x^4} \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = -\dfrac{3}{x^4} \)
\end{answerbox}
\textbf{Diagram.}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 06\quad\small\texttt{(d2-006)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 06 (d2-006)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Differentiate \( y = 5x^3 - 4x^2 + 7x - 2 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate each term separately.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3(5x^2) - 2(4x) + 7 - 0
\end{gathered}\]
The derivative of a sum is the sum of the derivatives. The constant \(-2\) differentiates to 0.

\textit{Step 2: Simplify.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 15x^2 - 8x + 7
\end{gathered}\]
Collect the simplified terms.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 15x^2 - 8x + 7 \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 07\quad\small\texttt{(d2-007)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 07 (d2-007)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 2}

\question{Differentiate \( f(x) = 2x^4 - 9x^2 + 1 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Differentiate each term.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = 8x^3 - 18x
\end{gathered}\]
The derivative of \(2x^4\) is \(8x^3\), of \(-9x^2\) is \(-18x\), and of the constant \(1\) is \(0\).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  f'(x) = 8x^3 - 18x \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 08\quad\small\texttt{(d2-008)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 08 (d2-008)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 2}

\question{Find \( \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} \) when \( y = 3x^{-2} + 5x \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate each term using the power rule.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3(-2)x^{-3} + 5(1)x^0
\end{gathered}\]
\(x^0 = 1\), so the derivative of \(5x\) is simply \(5\).

\textit{Step 2: Simplify.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = -6x^{-3} + 5
\end{gathered}\]
Or equivalently \( -\dfrac{6}{x^3} + 5 \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = -\dfrac{6}{x^3} + 5 \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 09\quad\small\texttt{(d2-009)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 09 (d2-009)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Expand and differentiate \( y = x(x - 3)^2 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Expand \( (x-3)^2 \) first.}
\[\begin{gathered}
(x-3)^2 = x^2 - 6x + 9
\end{gathered}\]
Square the bracket before multiplying.

\textit{Step 2: Multiply by \( x \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = x(x^2 - 6x + 9) = x^3 - 6x^2 + 9x
\end{gathered}\]
Distribute the \( x \) across every term.

\textit{Step 3: Differentiate term by term.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 - 12x + 9
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to each term.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 - 12x + 9 \)
\end{answerbox}
\textbf{Diagram.}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 10\quad\small\texttt{(d2-010)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 10 (d2-010)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Expand and differentiate \( y = (2x + 1)(x - 4) \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Expand the brackets.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = 2x^2 - 8x + x - 4 = 2x^2 - 7x - 4
\end{gathered}\]
FOIL: \( 2x \cdot x = 2x^2 \), \( 2x \cdot (-4) = -8x \), \( 1 \cdot x = x \), \( 1 \cdot (-4) = -4 \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 4x - 7
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to \(2x^2\) and \(-7x\); the constant \(-4\) disappears.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 4x - 7 \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 11\quad\small\texttt{(d2-011)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 11 (d2-011)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Find \( f'(x) \) for \( f(x) = \dfrac{x^3 + 4x}{x} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Split the fraction by dividing each term in the numerator by \( x \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
f(x) = \frac{x^3}{x} + \frac{4x}{x} = x^2 + 4
\end{gathered}\]
Simplify before differentiating --- never try to differentiate a fraction directly in this form.

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = 2x
\end{gathered}\]
The constant \(4\) differentiates to \(0\).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  f'(x) = 2x \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 12\quad\small\texttt{(d2-012)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 12 (d2-012)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Simplify and differentiate \( y = \dfrac{x^4 - 3x^2}{x^2} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Divide each term in the numerator by \( x^2 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = \frac{x^4}{x^2} - \frac{3x^2}{x^2} = x^2 - 3
\end{gathered}\]
This gives a simple polynomial to differentiate.

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x
\end{gathered}\]
The constant \(-3\) differentiates to \(0\).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 13\quad\small\texttt{(d2-013)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 13 (d2-013)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Differentiate \( y = 4\sqrt{x} - \dfrac{2}{x} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Rewrite using index notation.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = 4x^{1/2} - 2x^{-1}
\end{gathered}\]
\( \sqrt{x} = x^{1/2} \) and \( \dfrac{1}{x} = x^{-1} \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate each term.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 4 \cdot \tfrac{1}{2} x^{-1/2} - 2(-1)x^{-2} = 2x^{-1/2} + 2x^{-2}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to each term separately.

\textit{Step 3: Rewrite in standard form.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \frac{2}{\sqrt{x}} + \frac{2}{x^2}
\end{gathered}\]
Convert negative/fractional indices back to fractions or surds.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \dfrac{2}{\sqrt{x}} + \dfrac{2}{x^2} \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 14\quad\small\texttt{(d2-014)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 14 (d2-014)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Find the gradient of \( y = 3x^2 - 5x + 1 \) at \( x = 2 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate \( y \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 6x - 5
\end{gathered}\]
Power rule applied to each term.

\textit{Step 2: Substitute \( x = 2 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x}\bigg|_{x=2} = 6(2) - 5 = 12 - 5 = 7
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute the given \( x \)-value into the derivative.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad Gradient \(\displaystyle  = 7 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 15\quad\small\texttt{(d2-015)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 15 (d2-015)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Find the gradient of the curve \( y = 2x^3 - x \) at the point \( (1, 1) \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 6x^2 - 1
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to each term.

\textit{Step 2: Substitute \( x = 1 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 6(1)^2 - 1 = 5
\end{gathered}\]
The gradient at \( (1,1) \) is the value of the derivative when \( x = 1 \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad Gradient \(\displaystyle  = 5 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 16\quad\small\texttt{(d2-016)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 16 (d2-016)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Find the gradient of \( f(x) = 2\sqrt{x} \) at the point \( (9, 6) \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Rewrite \( f(x) \) using index notation.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f(x) = 2x^{1/2}
\end{gathered}\]
Convert the square root to a fractional power.

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = 2 \cdot \tfrac{1}{2} x^{-1/2} = x^{-1/2} = \frac{1}{\sqrt{x}}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule and simplify.

\textit{Step 3: Substitute \( x = 9 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(9) = \frac{1}{\sqrt{9}} = \frac{1}{3}
\end{gathered}\]
\( \sqrt{9} = 3 \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad Gradient \(\displaystyle  = \dfrac{1}{3} \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 17\quad\small\texttt{(d2-017)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 17 (d2-017)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Find the gradient of \( y = x^4 - 3x^2 + 2 \) when \( x = -1 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 4x^3 - 6x
\end{gathered}\]
Term-by-term differentiation.

\textit{Step 2: Substitute \( x = -1 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 4(-1)^3 - 6(-1) = -4 + 6 = 2
\end{gathered}\]
Be careful with negative values: \((-1)^3 = -1\).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad Gradient \(\displaystyle  = 2 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 18\quad\small\texttt{(d2-018)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 18 (d2-018)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the coordinates of the point on \( y = x^2 - 6x + 5 \) where the gradient is \( 0 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x - 6
\end{gathered}\]
The gradient function gives the slope at any point.

\textit{Step 2: Set the derivative equal to 0.}
\[\begin{gathered}
2x - 6 = 0 \implies x = 3
\end{gathered}\]
A gradient of 0 means the curve is neither rising nor falling at that point.

\textit{Step 3: Find the \( y \)-coordinate by substituting \( x = 3 \) into the original equation.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = (3)^2 - 6(3) + 5 = 9 - 18 + 5 = -4
\end{gathered}\]
Always substitute back into \( y \) (not \( \frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} \)) to find the \(y\)-coordinate.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  (3,\ -4) \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 19\quad\small\texttt{(d2-019)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 19 (d2-019)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the coordinates of the points on \( y = x^3 - 3x \) where the gradient is \( 0 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 - 3
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule term by term.

\textit{Step 2: Set equal to 0 and solve.}
\[\begin{gathered}
3x^2 - 3 = 0 \implies x^2 = 1 \implies x = \pm 1
\end{gathered}\]
Factorise or rearrange to find both values of \( x \).

\textit{Step 3: Find the \( y \)-coordinates.}
\[\begin{gathered}
x=1: y = 1 - 3 = -2; \quad x=-1: y = -1 + 3 = 2
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute each \( x \) back into \( y = x^3 - 3x \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  (1,\ -2) \) and \(\displaystyle  (-1,\ 2) \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 20\quad\small\texttt{(d2-020)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 20 (d2-020)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the equation of the tangent to \( y = x^2 + 3x \) at the point \( (1, 4) \). Give your answer in the form \( y = mx + c \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate to find the gradient function.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x + 3
\end{gathered}\]
The derivative gives the gradient of the tangent at any point.

\textit{Step 2: Find the gradient at \( x = 1 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
m = 2(1) + 3 = 5
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute \( x = 1 \) into the derivative.

\textit{Step 3: Use \( y - y_1 = m(x - x_1) \) with \( m = 5 \) and the point \( (1, 4) \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 4 = 5(x - 1) \implies y = 5x - 1
\end{gathered}\]
Rearrange to the required form \( y = mx + c \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  y = 5x - 1 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 21\quad\small\texttt{(d2-021)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 21 (d2-021)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the equation of the tangent to \( y = 2x^3 - 5 \) at the point \( (1, -3) \). Give your answer in the form \( ax + by + c = 0 \), where \( a \), \( b \), and \( c \) are integers.}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 6x^2
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to \( 2x^3 \); the constant \(-5\) differentiates to \(0\).

\textit{Step 2: Gradient at \( x = 1 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
m = 6(1)^2 = 6
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute \( x = 1 \).

\textit{Step 3: Equation of tangent.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - (-3) = 6(x - 1) \implies y = 6x - 9
\end{gathered}\]
Use point-slope form and expand.

\textit{Step 4: Rearrange to the required form.}
\[\begin{gathered}
6x - y - 9 = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Move everything to one side so coefficients are integers.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  6x - y - 9 = 0 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 22\quad\small\texttt{(d2-022)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 22 (d2-022)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the equation of the normal to \( y = x^2 - 4x + 3 \) at the point \( (3, 0) \). Give your answer in the form \( y = mx + c \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate to find the gradient of the curve.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x - 4
\end{gathered}\]
The derivative gives the gradient of the tangent at any point.

\textit{Step 2: Find the gradient of the tangent at \( x = 3 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
m_{\text{tangent}} = 2(3) - 4 = 2
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute \( x = 3 \) into the derivative.

\textit{Step 3: Find the gradient of the normal.}
\[\begin{gathered}
m_{\text{normal}} = -\frac{1}{2}
\end{gathered}\]
The normal is perpendicular to the tangent, so its gradient is the negative reciprocal: \( -\dfrac{1}{m} \).

\textit{Step 4: Write the equation of the normal through \( (3, 0) \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 0 = -\tfrac{1}{2}(x - 3) \implies y = -\tfrac{1}{2}x + \tfrac{3}{2}
\end{gathered}\]
Use \( y - y_1 = m(x - x_1) \) with the normal gradient and the given point.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  y = -\dfrac{1}{2}x + \dfrac{3}{2} \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 23\quad\small\texttt{(d2-023)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 23 (d2-023)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the equation of the normal to \( y = x^3 - 2x + 1 \) at the point \( (2, 5) \). Give your answer in the form \( ax + by + c = 0 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 - 2
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Gradient of tangent at \( x = 2 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
m_{\text{tangent}} = 3(4) - 2 = 10
\end{gathered}\]
\( 3(2)^2 - 2 = 12 - 2 = 10 \).

\textit{Step 3: Gradient of normal.}
\[\begin{gathered}
m_{\text{normal}} = -\frac{1}{10}
\end{gathered}\]
Negative reciprocal of \( 10 \).

\textit{Step 4: Equation of normal through \( (2, 5) \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 5 = -\tfrac{1}{10}(x - 2) \implies 10y - 50 = -(x - 2) \implies x + 10y - 52 = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Multiply through by 10 to clear the fraction, then rearrange.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  x + 10y - 52 = 0 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 24\quad\small\texttt{(d2-024)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 24 (d2-024)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Differentiate \( f(x) = (x+2)(x-5) \) and find the value of \( x \) where \( f'(x) = 3 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Expand the brackets.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f(x) = x^2 - 3x - 10
\end{gathered}\]
\( (x+2)(x-5) = x^2 - 5x + 2x - 10 \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = 2x - 3
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule term by term.

\textit{Step 3: Set \( f'(x) = 3 \) and solve.}
\[\begin{gathered}
2x - 3 = 3 \implies 2x = 6 \implies x = 3
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute the given gradient value and solve for \( x \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  x = 3 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 25\quad\small\texttt{(d2-025)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 25 (d2-025)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Differentiate \( y = \dfrac{x^3 - 5x^2 + 2x}{x} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Divide each term in the numerator by \( x \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = x^2 - 5x + 2
\end{gathered}\]
\( \dfrac{x^3}{x} = x^2 \), \( \dfrac{-5x^2}{x} = -5x \), \( \dfrac{2x}{x} = 2 \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x - 5
\end{gathered}\]
The constant \(2\) differentiates to \(0\).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x - 5 \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 26\quad\small\texttt{(d2-026)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 26 (d2-026)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find \( \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} \) when \( y = \dfrac{3x^3 + x}{\sqrt{x}} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Write \( \sqrt{x} = x^{1/2} \) and divide each numerator term.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = \frac{3x^3}{x^{1/2}} + \frac{x}{x^{1/2}} = 3x^{5/2} + x^{1/2}
\end{gathered}\]
Use the index law \( \dfrac{x^a}{x^b} = x^{a-b} \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3 \cdot \tfrac{5}{2} x^{3/2} + \tfrac{1}{2} x^{-1/2} = \tfrac{15}{2}x^{3/2} + \tfrac{1}{2}x^{-1/2}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to each term.

\textit{Step 3: Rewrite in a clean form.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \frac{15}{2}x\sqrt{x} + \frac{1}{2\sqrt{x}}
\end{gathered}\]
\( x^{3/2} = x \cdot \sqrt{x} \) and \( x^{-1/2} = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{x}} \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \dfrac{15x\sqrt{x}}{2} + \dfrac{1}{2\sqrt{x}} \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 27\quad\small\texttt{(d2-027)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 27 (d2-027)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Expand then differentiate \( y = (3x - 1)^2 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Expand the square.}
\[\begin{gathered}
(3x-1)^2 = 9x^2 - 6x + 1
\end{gathered}\]
\( (a - b)^2 = a^2 - 2ab + b^2 \) with \( a = 3x \), \( b = 1 \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 18x - 6
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to each term; the constant \(1\) differentiates to \(0\).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 18x - 6 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 28\quad\small\texttt{(d2-028)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 28 (d2-028)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the gradient of \( y = \dfrac{x^2 - 4}{x} \) at the point \( (-2, 0) \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Simplify by splitting the fraction.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = x - 4x^{-1}
\end{gathered}\]
\( \dfrac{x^2}{x} = x \) and \( \dfrac{-4}{x} = -4x^{-1} \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 1 + 4x^{-2} = 1 + \frac{4}{x^2}
\end{gathered}\]
Derivative of \( -4x^{-1} \) is \( +4x^{-2} \) (multiply by \(-1\), reduce index by 1).

\textit{Step 3: Substitute \( x = -2 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
1 + \frac{4}{(-2)^2} = 1 + \frac{4}{4} = 1 + 1 = 2
\end{gathered}\]
\( (-2)^2 = 4 \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad Gradient \(\displaystyle  = 2 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 29\quad\small\texttt{(d2-029)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 29 (d2-029)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Differentiate \( y = \sqrt{x}\,(x - 4) \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Rewrite and expand.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = x^{1/2}(x - 4) = x^{3/2} - 4x^{1/2}
\end{gathered}\]
Multiply \( x^{1/2} \) by each term: \( x^{1/2} \cdot x = x^{3/2} \) and \( x^{1/2} \cdot (-4) = -4x^{1/2} \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \tfrac{3}{2}x^{1/2} - 4 \cdot \tfrac{1}{2}x^{-1/2} = \tfrac{3}{2}\sqrt{x} - \frac{2}{\sqrt{x}}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to each fractional-index term.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \dfrac{3\sqrt{x}}{2} - \dfrac{2}{\sqrt{x}} \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 30\quad\small\texttt{(d2-030)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 30 (d2-030)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the equation of the tangent to \( y = (x-1)(x+3) \) at the point \( (2, 5) \). Give your answer in the form \( y = mx + c \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Expand the brackets.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = x^2 + 2x - 3
\end{gathered}\]
\( (x-1)(x+3) = x^2 + 3x - x - 3 \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x + 2
\end{gathered}\]
Power rule applied term by term.

\textit{Step 3: Gradient at \( x = 2 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
m = 2(2) + 2 = 6
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute \( x = 2 \) into the derivative.

\textit{Step 4: Equation of tangent through \( (2, 5) \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 5 = 6(x - 2) \implies y = 6x - 7
\end{gathered}\]
Expand and rearrange to \( y = mx + c \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  y = 6x - 7 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 31\quad\small\texttt{(d2-031)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 31 (d2-031)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 5}

\question{Find the equation of the normal to \( y = 4\sqrt{x} \) at the point \( (4, 8) \). Give your answer in the form \( ax + by + c = 0 \), where \( a \), \( b \), and \( c \) are integers.}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Write as a power of \( x \) and differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = 4x^{1/2} \implies \frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x^{-1/2} = \frac{2}{\sqrt{x}}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to \( 4x^{1/2} \).

\textit{Step 2: Gradient of tangent at \( x = 4 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
m_{\text{tangent}} = \frac{2}{\sqrt{4}} = \frac{2}{2} = 1
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute \( x = 4 \).

\textit{Step 3: Gradient of normal.}
\[\begin{gathered}
m_{\text{normal}} = -1
\end{gathered}\]
Negative reciprocal of \(1\) is \(-1\).

\textit{Step 4: Equation of normal through \( (4, 8) \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 8 = -1(x - 4) \implies y = -x + 12
\end{gathered}\]
Point-slope form with \( m = -1 \).

\textit{Step 5: Rearrange to integer form.}
\[\begin{gathered}
x + y - 12 = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Add \( x \) and subtract \( 12 \) from both sides.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  x + y - 12 = 0 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 32\quad\small\texttt{(d2-032)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 32 (d2-032)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 6}

\question{The curve \( C \) has equation \( y = x^3 - 4x + 3 \). (a) Find \( \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} \). (b) Show that the point \( P(2, 3) \) lies on \( C \). (c) Find the equation of the tangent to \( C \) at \( P \), giving your answer in the form \( y = mx + c \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: (a) Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 - 4
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to each term.

\textit{Step 2: (b) Substitute \( x = 2 \) into \( y = x^3 - 4x + 3 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = (2)^3 - 4(2) + 3 = 8 - 8 + 3 = 3 \checkmark
\end{gathered}\]
The point \( (2, 3) \) satisfies the equation, so it lies on \( C \).

\textit{Step 3: (c) Gradient at \( P(2, 3) \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
m = 3(2)^2 - 4 = 12 - 4 = 8
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute \( x = 2 \) into the derivative.

\textit{Step 4: Write the tangent equation.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 3 = 8(x - 2) \implies y = 8x - 13
\end{gathered}\]
Use point-slope form and rearrange.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad (a) \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 - 4 \) \\[3pt]  (b) \(\displaystyle  y = 8 - 8 + 3 = 3 \) $\checkmark$ \\[3pt]  (c) \(\displaystyle  y = 8x - 13 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 33\quad\small\texttt{(d2-033)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 33 (d2-033)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 6}

\question{The curve \( C \) has equation \( y = 2x^2 - 7x + 4 \). (a) Find the gradient of \( C \) at the point \( A(3, 1) \). (b) Hence find the equation of the normal to \( C \) at \( A \), giving your answer in the form \( ax + by + c = 0 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: (a) Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 4x - 7
\end{gathered}\]
Power rule applied to each term.

\textit{Step 2: Gradient at \( x = 3 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
m_{\text{tangent}} = 4(3) - 7 = 5
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute \( x = 3 \).

\textit{Step 3: (b) Gradient of normal.}
\[\begin{gathered}
m_{\text{normal}} = -\frac{1}{5}
\end{gathered}\]
The normal gradient is the negative reciprocal.

\textit{Step 4: Equation of normal through \( (3, 1) \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 1 = -\tfrac{1}{5}(x - 3) \implies 5y - 5 = -(x - 3) \implies x + 5y - 8 = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Multiply through by 5 to clear the fraction, then rearrange.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad (a) Gradient \(\displaystyle  = 5 \) \\[3pt]  (b) \(\displaystyle  x + 5y - 8 = 0 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 34\quad\small\texttt{(d2-034)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 34 (d2-034)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 8}

\question{The function \( f(x) = x^3 - 6x^2 + 9x + 2 \). (a) Find \( f'(x) \). (b) Find the coordinates of the two stationary points of the curve \( y = f(x) \). (c) Find the equation of the tangent to the curve at the point where \( x = 4 \). Give your answer in the form \( y = mx + c \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: (a) Differentiate \( f(x) \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = 3x^2 - 12x + 9
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to each term.

\textit{Step 2: (b) Set \( f'(x) = 0 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
3x^2 - 12x + 9 = 0 \implies x^2 - 4x + 3 = 0 \implies (x-1)(x-3) = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Divide through by 3, then factorise.

\textit{Step 3: So \( x = 1 \) or \( x = 3 \). Find the \( y \)-coordinates.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f(1) = 1 - 6 + 9 + 2 = 6; \quad f(3) = 27 - 54 + 27 + 2 = 2
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute each \( x \) back into \( f(x) \).

\textit{Step 4: (c) Gradient at \( x = 4 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(4) = 3(16) - 12(4) + 9 = 48 - 48 + 9 = 9
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute \( x = 4 \) into \( f'(x) \).

\textit{Step 5: Point on curve when \( x = 4 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
f(4) = 64 - 96 + 36 + 2 = 6
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute into \( f(x) \) to find the \( y \)-coordinate.

\textit{Step 6: Tangent at \( (4, 6) \) with gradient \( 9 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 6 = 9(x - 4) \implies y = 9x - 30
\end{gathered}\]
Use point-slope form and rearrange.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad (a) \(\displaystyle  f'(x) = 3x^2 - 12x + 9 \) \\[3pt]  (b) \(\displaystyle  (1,\, 6) \) and \(\displaystyle  (3,\, 2) \) \\[3pt]  (c) \(\displaystyle  y = 9x - 30 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 35\quad\small\texttt{(d2-035)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 35 (d2-035)}
\textit{Difficulty: Foundation \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 8}

\question{The curve \( C \) has equation \( y = f(x) \), where \( f(x) = \dfrac{x^3 - 4x}{\sqrt{x}} \) for \( x > 0 \). (a) Show that \( f(x) \) can be written in the form \( ax^p + bx^q \), stating the values of \( a \), \( p \), \( b \), and \( q \). (b) Find \( f'(x) \). (c) Find the equation of the tangent to \( C \) at the point where \( x = 4 \), giving your answer in the form \( y = mx + c \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: (a) Write \( \sqrt{x} = x^{1/2} \) and divide each term.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f(x) = \frac{x^3}{x^{1/2}} - \frac{4x}{x^{1/2}} = x^{5/2} - 4x^{1/2}
\end{gathered}\]
Use \( \dfrac{x^a}{x^b} = x^{a-b} \): \( x^{3-1/2} = x^{5/2} \) and \( x^{1-1/2} = x^{1/2} \).

\textit{Step 2: State the constants.}
\[\begin{gathered}
a = 1,\ p = \tfrac{5}{2},\ b = -4,\ q = \tfrac{1}{2}
\end{gathered}\]
Match the form \( ax^p + bx^q \).

\textit{Step 3: (b) Differentiate \( f(x) = x^{5/2} - 4x^{1/2} \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = \tfrac{5}{2}x^{3/2} - 4 \cdot \tfrac{1}{2}x^{-1/2} = \tfrac{5}{2}x^{3/2} - \frac{2}{\sqrt{x}}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to each term.

\textit{Step 4: (c) Find the \( y \)-coordinate when \( x = 4 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
f(4) = 4^{5/2} - 4 \cdot 4^{1/2} = 32 - 8 = 24
\end{gathered}\]
\( 4^{5/2} = (\sqrt{4})^5 = 2^5 = 32 \) and \( 4^{1/2} = 2 \).

\textit{Step 5: Gradient at \( x = 4 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(4) = \tfrac{5}{2}(4)^{3/2} - \frac{2}{\sqrt{4}} = \tfrac{5}{2}(8) - 1 = 20 - 1 = 19
\end{gathered}\]
\( 4^{3/2} = (\sqrt{4})^3 = 8 \).

\textit{Step 6: Tangent through \( (4, 24) \) with gradient \( 19 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 24 = 19(x - 4) \implies y = 19x - 52
\end{gathered}\]
Point-slope form, expanded and rearranged.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad (a) \(\displaystyle  f(x) = x^{5/2} - 4x^{1/2} \); \(\displaystyle  a=1, p=\tfrac{5}{2}, b=-4, q=\tfrac{1}{2} \) \\[3pt]  (b) \(\displaystyle  f'(x) = \dfrac{5}{2}x^{3/2} - \dfrac{2}{\sqrt{x}} \) \\[3pt]  (c) \(\displaystyle  y = 19x - 52 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 36\quad\small\texttt{(d2-036)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 36 (d2-036)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 2}

\question{Differentiate \( y = \dfrac{5}{x^2} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Rewrite with a negative index.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = 5x^{-2}
\end{gathered}\]
Bring the power to the numerator by negating the index.

\textit{Step 2: Apply the power rule.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = -10x^{-3} = -\dfrac{10}{x^3}
\end{gathered}\]
Multiply by the index (\( -2 \)) and reduce by 1.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = -\dfrac{10}{x^3} \)
\end{answerbox}
\textbf{Diagram.}

\begin{center}
\resizebox{\ifdim\width>\linewidth \linewidth\else \width\fi}{!}{%
\begin{tikzpicture}[>={Stealth[length=2mm]},font=\footnotesize,line cap=round,line join=round]
\begin{scope}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 37\quad\small\texttt{(d2-037)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 37 (d2-037)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 2}

\question{Differentiate \( f(x) = \dfrac{2}{\sqrt{x}} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Rewrite as a power.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f(x) = 2x^{-1/2}
\end{gathered}\]
\( \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{x}} = x^{-1/2} \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = 2 \cdot \left(-\tfrac{1}{2}\right) x^{-3/2} = -x^{-3/2}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 3: Rewrite with a surd.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = -\dfrac{1}{x\sqrt{x}}
\end{gathered}\]
\( x^{-3/2} = \dfrac{1}{x^{3/2}} = \dfrac{1}{x\sqrt{x}} \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  f'(x) = -\dfrac{1}{x\sqrt{x}} \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 38\quad\small\texttt{(d2-038)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 38 (d2-038)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 2}

\question{Differentiate \( y = 3\sqrt[3]{x} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Rewrite using a fractional index.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = 3x^{1/3}
\end{gathered}\]
\( \sqrt[3]{x} = x^{1/3} \).

\textit{Step 2: Apply the power rule.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3 \cdot \tfrac{1}{3} x^{-2/3} = x^{-2/3} = \dfrac{1}{x^{2/3}}
\end{gathered}\]
The coefficients cancel neatly.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt[3]{x^2}} \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 39\quad\small\texttt{(d2-039)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 39 (d2-039)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Differentiate \( y = 6x^{2/3} - 4x^{-1} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate term by term.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 6 \cdot \tfrac{2}{3} x^{-1/3} - 4 \cdot (-1) x^{-2}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to each term.

\textit{Step 2: Simplify.}
\[\begin{gathered}
= 4 x^{-1/3} + 4 x^{-2} = \dfrac{4}{\sqrt[3]{x}} + \dfrac{4}{x^2}
\end{gathered}\]
Rewrite with positive indices.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \dfrac{4}{\sqrt[3]{x}} + \dfrac{4}{x^2} \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 40\quad\small\texttt{(d2-040)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 40 (d2-040)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Differentiate \( f(x) = \dfrac{x^2 + 1}{x^3} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Split the fraction.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f(x) = \dfrac{x^2}{x^3} + \dfrac{1}{x^3} = x^{-1} + x^{-3}
\end{gathered}\]
Divide each numerator term by \( x^3 \) and write as powers.

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = -x^{-2} - 3x^{-4} = -\dfrac{1}{x^2} - \dfrac{3}{x^4}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to each term.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  f'(x) = -\dfrac{1}{x^2} - \dfrac{3}{x^4} \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 41\quad\small\texttt{(d2-041)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 41 (d2-041)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Differentiate \( y = \dfrac{2x^{3/2} - 5}{\sqrt{x}} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Split the fraction and simplify each term.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = \dfrac{2x^{3/2}}{x^{1/2}} - \dfrac{5}{x^{1/2}} = 2x - 5x^{-1/2}
\end{gathered}\]
Use the index law \( \dfrac{x^a}{x^b} = x^{a-b} \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2 + \tfrac{5}{2} x^{-3/2}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 3: Rewrite.}
\[\begin{gathered}
= 2 + \dfrac{5}{2x\sqrt{x}}
\end{gathered}\]
Convert the negative index back to a surd.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2 + \dfrac{5}{2x\sqrt{x}} \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 42\quad\small\texttt{(d2-042)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 42 (d2-042)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Differentiate \( y = (x^2 - 3)(x + 1) \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Expand the brackets.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = x^3 + x^2 - 3x - 3
\end{gathered}\]
Multiply term by term.

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 + 2x - 3
\end{gathered}\]
Differentiate each term; constants vanish.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 + 2x - 3 \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 43\quad\small\texttt{(d2-043)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 43 (d2-043)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Expand and differentiate \( y = (2x - 1)^2 (x + 3) \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Expand \( (2x - 1)^2 \) first.}
\[\begin{gathered}
(2x-1)^2 = 4x^2 - 4x + 1
\end{gathered}\]
Standard square expansion.

\textit{Step 2: Multiply by \( (x + 3) \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
(4x^2 - 4x + 1)(x + 3) = 4x^3 + 12x^2 - 4x^2 - 12x + x + 3 = 4x^3 + 8x^2 - 11x + 3
\end{gathered}\]
Expand term by term then combine like terms.

\textit{Step 3: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 12x^2 + 16x - 11
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 12x^2 + 16x - 11 \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 44\quad\small\texttt{(d2-044)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 44 (d2-044)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Differentiate \( y = \sqrt{x}(x^2 - 4) \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Expand using \( \sqrt{x} = x^{1/2} \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = x^{1/2} \cdot x^2 - 4x^{1/2} = x^{5/2} - 4x^{1/2}
\end{gathered}\]
Use the index law \( x^a \cdot x^b = x^{a+b} \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate term by term.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \tfrac{5}{2}x^{3/2} - 2x^{-1/2} = \tfrac{5}{2}x\sqrt{x} - \dfrac{2}{\sqrt{x}}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule and convert back to surd form.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \dfrac{5}{2}x\sqrt{x} - \dfrac{2}{\sqrt{x}} \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 45\quad\small\texttt{(d2-045)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 45 (d2-045)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Differentiate \( f(x) = \dfrac{(x + 2)(x - 1)}{x^2} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Expand the numerator.}
\[\begin{gathered}
(x+2)(x-1) = x^2 + x - 2
\end{gathered}\]
Standard expansion.

\textit{Step 2: Divide each term by \( x^2 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
f(x) = 1 + x^{-1} - 2x^{-2}
\end{gathered}\]
Write each quotient as a power of \( x \).

\textit{Step 3: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = -x^{-2} + 4x^{-3} = -\dfrac{1}{x^2} + \dfrac{4}{x^3}
\end{gathered}\]
Constants vanish; apply the power rule to the remaining terms.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  f'(x) = -\dfrac{1}{x^2} + \dfrac{4}{x^3} \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 46\quad\small\texttt{(d2-046)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 46 (d2-046)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Differentiate \( y = \left( x + \dfrac{1}{x} \right)^2 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Expand the square.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = x^2 + 2 + \dfrac{1}{x^2} = x^2 + 2 + x^{-2}
\end{gathered}\]
Use \( (a+b)^2 = a^2 + 2ab + b^2 \); note that \( x \cdot \dfrac{1}{x} = 1 \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x - 2x^{-3} = 2x - \dfrac{2}{x^3}
\end{gathered}\]
Constant disappears; apply the power rule.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x - \dfrac{2}{x^3} \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 47\quad\small\texttt{(d2-047)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 47 (d2-047)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Differentiate \( y = \dfrac{(\sqrt{x} + 1)^2}{x} \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Expand the numerator.}
\[\begin{gathered}
(\sqrt{x} + 1)^2 = x + 2\sqrt{x} + 1
\end{gathered}\]
\( (\sqrt{x})^2 = x \).

\textit{Step 2: Divide each term by \( x \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = 1 + 2x^{-1/2} + x^{-1}
\end{gathered}\]
\( \dfrac{\sqrt{x}}{x} = x^{-1/2} \).

\textit{Step 3: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = -x^{-3/2} - x^{-2} = -\dfrac{1}{x\sqrt{x}} - \dfrac{1}{x^2}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = -\dfrac{1}{x\sqrt{x}} - \dfrac{1}{x^2} \)
\end{answerbox}

\medskip\hrule

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 48\quad\small\texttt{(d2-048)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 48 (d2-048)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Differentiate \( f(x) = (x^2 + 2x)(x - 3) \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Expand the brackets.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f(x) = x^3 - 3x^2 + 2x^2 - 6x = x^3 - x^2 - 6x
\end{gathered}\]
Collect like terms.

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = 3x^2 - 2x - 6
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  f'(x) = 3x^2 - 2x - 6 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 49\quad\small\texttt{(d2-049)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 49 (d2-049)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Find the gradient of the curve \( y = x^3 - 2x^2 + 5 \) at the point where \( x = -1 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 - 4x
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Substitute \( x = -1 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
3(-1)^2 - 4(-1) = 3 + 4 = 7
\end{gathered}\]
Evaluate the gradient function.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad Gradient at \(\displaystyle  x = -1 \) is \(\displaystyle  7 \).
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 50\quad\small\texttt{(d2-050)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 50 (d2-050)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Find the gradient of \( y = \dfrac{4}{x} + x^2 \) at the point where \( x = 2 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Rewrite and differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = 4x^{-1} + x^2 \;\Rightarrow\; \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = -4x^{-2} + 2x
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule to each term.

\textit{Step 2: Substitute \( x = 2 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
-\dfrac{4}{4} + 4 = -1 + 4 = 3
\end{gathered}\]
Evaluate.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad Gradient at \(\displaystyle  x = 2 \) is \(\displaystyle  3 \).
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 51\quad\small\texttt{(d2-051)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 51 (d2-051)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the gradient of \( f(x) = \sqrt{x}(x + 2) \) at the point where \( x = 4 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Expand.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f(x) = x^{3/2} + 2x^{1/2}
\end{gathered}\]
Write as powers of \( x \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = \tfrac{3}{2}x^{1/2} + x^{-1/2} = \tfrac{3}{2}\sqrt{x} + \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{x}}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 3: Substitute \( x = 4 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(4) = \tfrac{3}{2}(2) + \tfrac{1}{2} = 3 + \tfrac{1}{2} = \tfrac{7}{2}
\end{gathered}\]
\( \sqrt{4} = 2 \), \( \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{4}} = \dfrac{1}{2} \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad Gradient at \(\displaystyle  x = 4 \) is \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{7}{2} \).
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 52\quad\small\texttt{(d2-052)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 52 (d2-052)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{The curve \( C \) has equation \( y = x^3 - 4x \). Find the values of \( x \) at which the gradient of \( C \) is \( 8 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 - 4
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Set gradient equal to 8.}
\[\begin{gathered}
3x^2 - 4 = 8 \;\Rightarrow\; 3x^2 = 12 \;\Rightarrow\; x^2 = 4
\end{gathered}\]
Rearrange and solve.

\textit{Step 3: Solve.}
\[\begin{gathered}
x = \pm 2
\end{gathered}\]
Two values of \( x \).

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  x = 2 \) or \(\displaystyle  x = -2 \).
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 53\quad\small\texttt{(d2-053)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 53 (d2-053)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the coordinates of the point on \( y = 2x^2 - 3x + 1 \) where the gradient is \( 5 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 4x - 3
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Set the derivative equal to 5.}
\[\begin{gathered}
4x - 3 = 5 \;\Rightarrow\; x = 2
\end{gathered}\]
Solve for \( x \).

\textit{Step 3: Find \( y \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = 2(4) - 3(2) + 1 = 8 - 6 + 1 = 3
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute into the original equation.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad Point is \(\displaystyle  (2, 3) \).
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 54\quad\small\texttt{(d2-054)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 54 (d2-054)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the equation of the tangent to \( y = x^3 - 2x \) at the point \( (2, 4) \). Give your answer in the form \( y = mx + c \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 - 2
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Evaluate at \( x = 2 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
3(4) - 2 = 10
\end{gathered}\]
The tangent has gradient 10.

\textit{Step 3: Use point-slope form.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 4 = 10(x - 2) \;\Rightarrow\; y = 10x - 16
\end{gathered}\]
Expand and rearrange.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  y = 10x - 16 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 55\quad\small\texttt{(d2-055)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 55 (d2-055)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the equation of the tangent to \( y = \dfrac{6}{x} \) at the point where \( x = 3 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Find the \( y \)-coordinate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = \dfrac{6}{3} = 2 \;\Rightarrow\; (3, 2)
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute \( x = 3 \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = 6x^{-1} \;\Rightarrow\; \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = -6x^{-2} = -\dfrac{6}{x^2}
\end{gathered}\]
Use the power rule.

\textit{Step 3: Gradient at \( x = 3 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
-\dfrac{6}{9} = -\dfrac{2}{3}
\end{gathered}\]
Evaluate the derivative.

\textit{Step 4: Tangent equation.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 2 = -\tfrac{2}{3}(x - 3) \;\Rightarrow\; y = -\tfrac{2}{3}x + 4
\end{gathered}\]
Point-slope, then rearrange.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  y = -\dfrac{2}{3}x + 4 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 56\quad\small\texttt{(d2-056)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 56 (d2-056)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the equation of the normal to \( y = x^2 + 2x - 3 \) at the point \( (1, 0) \). Give your answer in the form \( y = mx + c \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x + 2
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Gradient of tangent at \( (1,0) \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
2(1) + 2 = 4
\end{gathered}\]
Evaluate the derivative.

\textit{Step 3: Gradient of normal.}
\[\begin{gathered}
m_{\text{normal}} = -\dfrac{1}{4}
\end{gathered}\]
Perpendicular: \( m_1 m_2 = -1 \).

\textit{Step 4: Normal equation.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 0 = -\tfrac{1}{4}(x - 1) \;\Rightarrow\; y = -\tfrac{1}{4}x + \tfrac{1}{4}
\end{gathered}\]
Point-slope, then rearrange.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  y = -\dfrac{1}{4}x + \dfrac{1}{4} \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 57\quad\small\texttt{(d2-057)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 57 (d2-057)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{The curve \( C \) has equation \( y = \sqrt{x} \). Find the equation of the tangent to \( C \) at the point where \( x = 9 \). Give your answer in the form \( y = mx + c \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Find the \( y \)-coordinate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = \sqrt{9} = 3 \;\Rightarrow\; (9, 3)
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute \( x = 9 \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = x^{1/2} \;\Rightarrow\; \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \tfrac{1}{2}x^{-1/2} = \dfrac{1}{2\sqrt{x}}
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 3: Gradient at \( x = 9 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{1}{2 \cdot 3} = \tfrac{1}{6}
\end{gathered}\]
Evaluate.

\textit{Step 4: Tangent equation.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 3 = \tfrac{1}{6}(x - 9) \;\Rightarrow\; y = \tfrac{1}{6}x + \tfrac{3}{2}
\end{gathered}\]
Point-slope, then rearrange.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  y = \dfrac{1}{6}x + \dfrac{3}{2} \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 58\quad\small\texttt{(d2-058)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 58 (d2-058)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 5}

\question{Find the equation of the normal to \( y = 2x^3 - 3x^2 + 1 \) at the point where \( x = 1 \). Give your answer in the form \( ax + by + c = 0 \) with integer coefficients.}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Find the \( y \)-coordinate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = 2 - 3 + 1 = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; (1, 0)
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute \( x = 1 \).

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 6x^2 - 6x
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 3: Tangent gradient at \( x = 1 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
6 - 6 = 0
\end{gathered}\]
The tangent is horizontal.

\textit{Step 4: Normal is vertical.}
\[\begin{gathered}
x = 1 \;\Leftrightarrow\; x - 1 = 0
\end{gathered}\]
A line perpendicular to a horizontal tangent is vertical.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  x - 1 = 0 \)
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 59\quad\small\texttt{(d2-059)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 59 (d2-059)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{Find the coordinates of the stationary point of \( y = x^2 - 8x + 5 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 2x - 8
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Set gradient to zero.}
\[\begin{gathered}
2x - 8 = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; x = 4
\end{gathered}\]
Solve for \( x \).

\textit{Step 3: Find \( y \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = 16 - 32 + 5 = -11
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad Stationary point at \(\displaystyle  (4, -11) \).
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 60\quad\small\texttt{(d2-060)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 60 (d2-060)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 5}

\question{Find the coordinates of the stationary points of \( f(x) = x^3 - 3x^2 - 9x + 1 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
f'(x) = 3x^2 - 6x - 9
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Solve \( f'(x) = 0 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
3x^2 - 6x - 9 = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; x^2 - 2x - 3 = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; (x-3)(x+1) = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Factorise.

\textit{Step 3: Values of \( x \) and corresponding \( y \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
x = 3: \; f(3) = 27 - 27 - 27 + 1 = -26 \\ x = -1: \; f(-1) = -1 - 3 + 9 + 1 = 6
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute each value.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad Stationary points at \(\displaystyle  (3, -26) \) and \(\displaystyle  (-1, 6) \).
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 61\quad\small\texttt{(d2-061)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 61 (d2-061)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{The curve \( y = 2x^3 + 3x^2 - 12x + 5 \) has two stationary points. Find their \( x \)-coordinates.}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 6x^2 + 6x - 12
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Set to zero and simplify.}
\[\begin{gathered}
6x^2 + 6x - 12 = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; x^2 + x - 2 = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Divide by 6.

\textit{Step 3: Factorise and solve.}
\[\begin{gathered}
(x + 2)(x - 1) = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; x = -2 \text{ or } x = 1
\end{gathered}\]
Two stationary points.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  x = -2 \) and \(\displaystyle  x = 1 \).
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 62\quad\small\texttt{(d2-062)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 62 (d2-062)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 5}

\question{The curve \( y = 4x^3 - 12x + 7 \) has a tangent parallel to the \( x \)-axis. Find the \( y \)-coordinates of the points where this occurs.}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 12x^2 - 12
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Tangent parallel to \( x \)-axis when gradient = 0.}
\[\begin{gathered}
12x^2 - 12 = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; x^2 = 1 \;\Rightarrow\; x = \pm 1
\end{gathered}\]
Solve for \( x \).

\textit{Step 3: Find \( y \) at each value.}
\[\begin{gathered}
x = 1: \; 4 - 12 + 7 = -1 \\ x = -1: \; -4 + 12 + 7 = 15
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute into the original equation.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  y = -1 \) and \(\displaystyle  y = 15 \).
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 63\quad\small\texttt{(d2-063)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 63 (d2-063)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{Find the values of \( x \) for which the curve \( y = x^3 - 3x^2 + 4 \) has gradient \( 9 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 - 6x
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Set equal to 9.}
\[\begin{gathered}
3x^2 - 6x = 9 \;\Rightarrow\; x^2 - 2x - 3 = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Rearrange to a quadratic in \( x \).

\textit{Step 3: Factorise and solve.}
\[\begin{gathered}
(x - 3)(x + 1) = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; x = 3 \text{ or } x = -1
\end{gathered}\]
Two solutions.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  x = 3 \) or \(\displaystyle  x = -1 \).
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 64\quad\small\texttt{(d2-064)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 64 (d2-064)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{The volume \( V \) cm\( ^3 \) of a cube of side \( x \) cm is \( V = x^3 \). Find \( \dfrac{\mathrm{d}V}{\mathrm{d}x} \) and evaluate it when \( x = 5 \). Interpret your answer.}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}V}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Substitute \( x = 5 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
3(25) = 75
\end{gathered}\]
Value of the derivative.

\textit{Step 3: Interpret.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}V}{\mathrm{d}x}\bigg|_{x=5} = 75 \text{ cm}^3 / \text{cm}
\end{gathered}\]
When the side is 5 cm, the volume is increasing at 75 cm\textsuperscript{3} per unit increase in side length.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}V}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 \); at \(\displaystyle  x = 5 \) it equals \(\displaystyle  75 \) cm\textsuperscript{3} per cm.
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 65\quad\small\texttt{(d2-065)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 65 (d2-065)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 4}

\question{The displacement \( s \) metres of a particle at time \( t \) seconds is \( s = t^3 - 6t^2 + 9t \). Find the velocity when \( t = 2 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate to get velocity.}
\[\begin{gathered}
v = \dfrac{\mathrm{d}s}{\mathrm{d}t} = 3t^2 - 12t + 9
\end{gathered}\]
Velocity is the derivative of displacement.

\textit{Step 2: Substitute \( t = 2 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
3(4) - 12(2) + 9 = 12 - 24 + 9 = -3
\end{gathered}\]
Evaluate.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad Velocity at \(\displaystyle  t = 2 \) is \(\displaystyle  -3 \) m/s.
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 66\quad\small\texttt{(d2-066)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 66 (d2-066)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{The cost \( C \) (in \pounds ) of producing \( x \) widgets is \( C = 200 + 5x + 0.01x^2 \). Find the marginal cost \( \dfrac{\mathrm{d}C}{\mathrm{d}x} \) when \( x = 100 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}C}{\mathrm{d}x} = 5 + 0.02x
\end{gathered}\]
Constant \( 200 \) vanishes.

\textit{Step 2: Substitute \( x = 100 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
5 + 2 = 7
\end{gathered}\]
Marginal cost at 100 widgets.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad Marginal cost is \pounds 7 per widget.
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 67\quad\small\texttt{(d2-067)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 67 (d2-067)}
\textit{Difficulty: Standard \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 3}

\question{A spherical balloon has volume \( V = \dfrac{4}{3}\pi r^3 \) cm\( ^3 \), where \( r \) is its radius in cm. Find \( \dfrac{\mathrm{d}V}{\mathrm{d}r} \) and evaluate it when \( r = 10 \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: Differentiate with respect to \( r \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}V}{\mathrm{d}r} = \dfrac{4}{3}\pi \cdot 3r^2 = 4\pi r^2
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Substitute \( r = 10 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
4\pi (100) = 400\pi
\end{gathered}\]
Evaluate.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}V}{\mathrm{d}r} = 4\pi r^2 \); at \(\displaystyle  r = 10 \) it equals \(\displaystyle  400\pi \) cm\textsuperscript{3} per cm.
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 68\quad\small\texttt{(d2-068)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 68 (d2-068)}
\textit{Difficulty: Challenge \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 6}

\question{The curve \( C \) has equation \( y = x^3 - 6x^2 + 11x - 4 \).\\[3pt]  (a) Find \( \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} \).\\[3pt]  (b) The tangent at the point \( P \) on \( C \) is parallel to the line \( y = 2x + 1 \). Find the \( x \)-coordinates of the possible positions of \( P \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: (a) Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 - 12x + 11
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: (b) Parallel to \( y = 2x + 1 \) means gradient is 2.}
\[\begin{gathered}
3x^2 - 12x + 11 = 2
\end{gathered}\]
Set derivative equal to the target gradient.

\textit{Step 3: Rearrange.}
\[\begin{gathered}
3x^2 - 12x + 9 = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; x^2 - 4x + 3 = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Divide by 3.

\textit{Step 4: Factorise and solve.}
\[\begin{gathered}
(x - 1)(x - 3) = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; x = 1 \text{ or } x = 3
\end{gathered}\]
Two possible positions.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad (a) \(\displaystyle  \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 - 12x + 11 \) \\[3pt]  (b) \(\displaystyle  x = 1 \) or \(\displaystyle  x = 3 \).
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 69\quad\small\texttt{(d2-069)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 69 (d2-069)}
\textit{Difficulty: Challenge \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 7}

\question{The curve \( C \) has equation \( y = 2\sqrt{x} + \dfrac{8}{\sqrt{x}} \) for \( x > 0 \).\\[3pt]  (a) Show that \( \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = \dfrac{x - 4}{x\sqrt{x}} \).\\[3pt]  (b) Find the coordinates of the stationary point of \( C \).}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: (a) Write with fractional indices.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = 2x^{1/2} + 8x^{-1/2}
\end{gathered}\]
Prepare each term for the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = x^{-1/2} - 4x^{-3/2}
\end{gathered}\]
Multiply each term by its index and reduce by 1.

\textit{Step 3: Factor out \( x^{-3/2} \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
= x^{-3/2}(x - 4) = \dfrac{x - 4}{x\sqrt{x}} \checkmark
\end{gathered}\]
Matches the required form.

\textit{Step 4: (b) Stationary when numerator is zero.}
\[\begin{gathered}
x - 4 = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; x = 4
\end{gathered}\]
Denominator non-zero for \( x > 0 \).

\textit{Step 5: Find \( y \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y = 2\sqrt{4} + \dfrac{8}{\sqrt{4}} = 4 + 4 = 8
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute back.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad (a) Shown. \\[3pt]  (b) Stationary point at \(\displaystyle  (4, 8) \).
\end{answerbox}

\section*{Differentiating y = f(x) 70\quad\small\texttt{(d2-070)}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Differentiating y = f(x) 70 (d2-070)}
\textit{Difficulty: Challenge \quad\textbar\quad Marks: 9}

\question{The curve \( C \) has equation \( y = x^3 - 3x^2 - 24x + 1 \).\\[3pt]  (a) Find the coordinates of the two stationary points of \( C \).\\[3pt]  (b) Find the equation of the tangent to \( C \) at the point where \( x = 0 \), giving your answer in the form \( y = mx + c \).\\[3pt]  (c) Determine the values of \( x \) for which \( C \) has gradient greater than 0.}

\textbf{Worked solution.}

\textit{Step 1: (a) Differentiate.}
\[\begin{gathered}
\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x} = 3x^2 - 6x - 24
\end{gathered}\]
Apply the power rule.

\textit{Step 2: Solve gradient = 0.}
\[\begin{gathered}
3(x^2 - 2x - 8) = 0 \;\Rightarrow\; (x-4)(x+2) = 0
\end{gathered}\]
Factor out 3, then factorise the quadratic.

\textit{Step 3: Compute \( y \) values.}
\[\begin{gathered}
x = 4: \; 64 - 48 - 96 + 1 = -79 \\ x = -2: \; -8 - 12 + 48 + 1 = 29
\end{gathered}\]
Substitute into the original equation.

\textit{Step 4: (b) Tangent at \( x = 0 \).}
\[\begin{gathered}
y(0) = 1, \quad \dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}x}(0) = -24
\end{gathered}\]
Evaluate the curve and gradient at 0.

\textit{Step 5: Tangent equation.}
\[\begin{gathered}
y - 1 = -24(x - 0) \;\Rightarrow\; y = -24x + 1
\end{gathered}\]
Point-slope form.

\textit{Step 6: (c) Curve increasing where derivative > 0.}
\[\begin{gathered}
(x-4)(x+2) > 0 \;\Rightarrow\; x < -2 \text{ or } x > 4
\end{gathered}\]
Quadratic positive outside its roots.

\begin{answerbox}
\textbf{Final answer:}\quad (a) \(\displaystyle  (-2, 29) \) and \(\displaystyle  (4, -79) \) \\[3pt]  (b) \(\displaystyle  y = -24x + 1 \) \\[3pt]  (c) \(\displaystyle  x < -2 \) or \(\displaystyle  x > 4 \).
\end{answerbox}
